# ICT Mentorship bot: consolidated rulebook

> `mentorship/digests/m2024_draft_rules.md` (notes: `mentorship/notes_2024/`). Nothing there
> overrides this file until Mase accepts it.

> **Owner corrections 2026-10-18** (`mentorship/digests/owner_directives.md`,
> `owner_directive_specs.md`) override this file where they differ: last hour 15:00-15:50 and the
> 16:15 PM close (#1); 5 handles = minimum room (#2); year end from December's direction (#3,
> pending); news dealing-range wait (#4); bearish unicorn (#5); breaker MT respect (#6); opposing
> HTF FVG = resistance (#7); close (not tighten) before noon (#8); lock one draw per session (#9);
> stand aside on sloppy days (#10, pending); OB entry before the break (#11); 1.2% / 1.3% risk
> (#12); front-month prices (#13); full-range breaker (#14). Built status: `digests/build_log.md`.

> `mentorship/digests/te_charter_draft_rules.md` (notes: `mentorship/notes_te_charter/`). Nothing there
> overrides this file until Mase accepts it.

## 1. Header

**Scope.** One rulebook for the ICT Mentorship bot (`mentorship/`), covering the 2023 Mentorship
playlist (notes 001-105, with 065 split into 065A/065B) and Core Content Months 01-12 (2016-17,
notes cc01_* to cc12_*). The bot is backtest and paper only. It reads signals from HistData
SPX/NDX 1-minute index CFD bars (New York time; RTH is the focus, but the files also carry
overnight bars, so a real midnight open, London range and overnight high/low can be computed).
It trades SPY/QQQ through Schwab, which cannot trade futures.

**Sources.** Seven topic digests in `mentorship/digests/` (models, pd_arrays, times, filters,
execution, levels, bias), which condense all 218 per-video notes in `mentorship/notes/`, plus the
caption-verified Ep 01-08 rules (Appendix A). Go to the digests for the full source lists per rule
and to the notes for context, charts and trade examples. This file states each rule once.

**Notation.**
- Citations: `(023 ~12:30; cc04_005)` = note file id and approximate video time. "Ep 0x" and
  "rules Ep 0x" refer to Appendix A.
- Tags: **[2023]** = 2023 playlist, **[CC]** = Core Content, **[both]**; **[chart]** = only seen on
  charts; **[fx-only]** / **[bonds-only]** = does not transfer to index futures as stated;
  **{needs: X}** = data the pipeline lacks; **{d: X}** = free daily data exists but is not loaded.
- **Bot:** = what `strategy.py` / `levels.py` / `backtest.py` do today. **D#** = see Decision # in
  section 3. **A#** = definition rule in 2.1. **P1-P3** = proposed change in section 4.
- Units: ICT quotes ES points ("handles"). 5 ES pts = 20 NQ pts, so NQ/NDX thresholds are about 4x
  ES (049 ~2:44; 071 ~20:34). SPX pts are about ES pts; SPY is about SPX/10 (`Params.es_pt`).
  "1 tick" = 0.25 ES pt.
- All times New York local time, daylight saving included. Bars are used only after they close.

---

## 2. Bot rulebook

### 2.1 Definitions: PD arrays and structure

**Swings and liquidity**
- **A1 Swing point** = 3-candle pivot: middle high above both neighbours (low below both); not the
  5-candle Williams fractal (012 ~30:08; 050 ~6:02; cc01_004 ~11:56; cc01_005 ~11:49) [both].
  Hierarchy: STL = plain swing; ITL = low with a higher STL on each side; LTL = low with higher
  ITLs on each side (014 ~0:12; cc05_002 ~20:14) [both]. A swing high is "confirmed" once a later
  candle trades lower (cc01_004 ~9:44) [CC]. Bot: `pivot_n=1` (3-candle, default; 2 = the
  5-candle switch, D17); ITL/LTL tiers `levels.swing_tiers`, MSS reference tier `mss_ref`.
  - Caption-gap fix (A1-impulse-swing-unit; cc01_008 ~1:52): the unit is the IMPULSE swing - a clear one-directional leg between alternating swing extremes; keep only clear swings (item 54's test: >= 60% of bars in the direction, or a displacement FVG inside, or >= 1x ATR). The fib reference is the active parent leg, not the latest minor leg (FX only: protraction-time manipulation legs). Bot: `levels.impulse_legs` / `leg_is_impulsive` (helper, no switch; the A21 `leg_anchor="energetic"` anchor uses the same test).
- **A2 Buy-side liquidity (BSL)** = buy stops above old highs and relative equal highs;
  **sell-side (SSL)** = sell stops below old lows and relative equal lows (001 ~13:02; cc01_007
  ~1:09; cc04_011 ~4:11) [both]. A level is "booked" only when price trades 1 tick through it
  (042 ~59:04) [2023]. An imbalance is not liquidity (033 ~24:34) [2023].
  - Caption-gap fix (A2-which-old-high; 002 ~0:34): the active buy-side pool = the first earlier HTF (daily) swing high above today's high so far, walking back from the newest to the oldest swing high; the sell side mirrors it below today's low. Bot: `active_old_pool` (off) = pools old_hi / old_lo (targets, sweep pools, lock candidates).
  - Caption-gap fix (A2-round-number-pool; 002 ~2:37): a round 00 handle above (below) price acts as buy-side (sell-side); a sweep = trading through it. Use it as CONFLUENCE for the sweep step alongside an old high / low (within 1 ES pt [our interpretation]), never on its own. Bot: `round_pool_mode` "pool" (the built standalone round pools, owner #13) / "confluence" (a handle is kept only with an old high / low (daily, prior week / month, the active old pool), a rel-eq or an engineered pool within `round_conf_pts`; tag `round_conf`) / "tag_only".
  - Caption-gap fix (A2-open-float; 033 ~24:26, cc05_002 ~1:10, cc09_004 ~4:02): open float = all resting stop orders around price, not only at old highs / lows - possibly just beyond price. After a down move the short-term highs (REH) formed during the decline hold shorts' trailed stops: when price is denied continuation (a small, choppy range: no new low for `float_denied_bars` = 20 bars with a range under `float_denied_adr` = 0.3 x ADR5 [our interpretation]) the draw is the nearest such pool above; mirror. In a consolidation the pools sit just beyond its high / low and at its internal highs / lows, heavier with duration (L4; day-trade consolidations are brief). Bot: `open_float_pools` (off; the duration weight is not built).
  - Caption-gap fix (A2-delivery-vs-liquidity; 104 ~11:24: "buy side delivery is the actual animated movement of price"): buy-side DELIVERY = a leg moving higher, sell-side delivery = a leg moving lower; buy-side / sell-side LIQUIDITY = the static stop pools (above). The MM sell model: buy-side delivery runs into BSL (REH), then sell-side delivery runs to the new SSL engineered below the rally's lows. Bot: `Trade.delivery` ("up" / "down" = the last displacement leg, always tagged), `mm_ssl_target` (off; with `mmxm_ctx` the draw is the far end of the delivery leg - side space: the highest high of the leg from `leg_origin` into the sweep extreme).
  - Caption-gap fix (A2-quick-run-through; 033 ~51:37): 1 tick through books the level; continuation toward the next draw wants a fast run through it with expansion and speed, not a tap (bot proxy: more than 1 tick beyond within N bars, N 1-3 - ours). A tap and stall = a reaction point. Bot: `run_through_bars` (0 = off) / `run_through_ticks` (tag `rt_stall`; a later body close back through the rung exits the rest).
- **A3 Relative equal highs/lows (REH/REL)** = 2+ highs (lows) close together; the smoother, the
  stronger the magnet; they get run even under an older higher high (Ep 04; 006 ~26:43; 025
  ~36:20; cc01_003 ~15:23; cc04_014 ~2:17) [both]. Nearly level bodies count even if wicks differ
  (cc05_003 ~1:07:26); draw the REL line at the lower of the two lows (038 ~43:43). ICT never gives
  a tolerance. Bot: `eq_tol_pts=0.5` ES pt, cluster extreme = the pool.
  - Caption-gap fix (A3-diagonal-support-is-liquidity; 045 ~26:02, cc03_007 ~2:12/~23:18): never use a diagonal trendline or a pattern breakout as S/R, bias, entry or stop. A smooth diagonal run of higher lows (lower highs) on the execution TF = retail stops beneath (above): expect a spike through it; a trendline/pattern break against daily order flow is a trap. Bot: `diag_pools` (off; >= `diag_min_swings` = 3 swings within the REL tolerance of one line [our interpretation]) = a sweep pool of kind "diag" at the extrapolated line.
  - Caption-gap fix (A3-engineered-liquidity; 035 ~49:54): engineered buy-side = an old swing high H1, a later LOWER high H2 that falls short of it (within `eng_short_pts` = 3 ES pts [our interpretation]), then price drops away (a swing low below the low between them): the new shorts' buy stops rest above H2 and H1, so the pool is above H1; rank it higher with more time between H1 and H2 and with an FVG above H1. Mirror for sell-side. Bot: `engineered_pools` (off; 15m / 1h / daily swings; counts like a rel-eq pool under `eq_pref` in the sweep pick and the draw, higher score first among engineered sweeps; the FVG bonus uses an opposing FVG known by the pool's creation bar; a sweep of one is tagged `engineered`).
  - Caption-gap fix (A3-clean-high; cc01_003 ~15:33): a clean high = swing high H1, price trades away for a while, then H2 within the tolerance of H1 (slightly below OR ABOVE); buy stops sit above max(H1, H2). The two swings must be separated (>= `eq_min_sep_bars` bars; two adjacent equal bars are one swing) [our interpretation]. Bot: `eq_min_sep_bars` (0 = the old rule; 3 to test), the pool is the cluster's most extreme price and a slightly higher second high no longer spends the first.
  - Caption-gap fix (LLT3-equal-highs-fade-condition; cc09_005 ~20:04): after price runs an REH, short the raid if (1) the HTF order flow (weekly / daily / 4h) is bearish and the rally is a retracement into a premium array, or (2) the market is neutral / consolidating or at a turning point and an HTF premium array sits just above the REH; if neither, the REH is a continuation TARGET. Mirror for REL. Index depth up to ~10 handles (L4) is our interpretation. Bot (off): `reh_fade_gate`.
  - Caption-gap fix (A3-reh-under-older-high; 021 ~00:40:41: "if it's going to go to 4089.75 it's going to rip to 4092"): a REH within ~2 ES pts under an older swing high is ONE buy-side pool; the target is just beyond the older high (X2: +0.75 pt), not the REH; sweeping the REH alone does not book the merged pool. Mirror for a REL above an older lower low. [our interpretation] the 2-pt merge distance. Bot (off): `reh_merge_pts` (2.0 / 3.0 test values).
  - Caption-gap fix (new-defended-level; cc01_007 ~00:24:23: "that is indicating a levels being defended"): the inverse of a clean level - a level whose band (0.1 x ADR5 [our interpretation]) was touched at least 4 times in the lookback is "defended": not a target, and trades go away from it (longs off a defended low, shorts off a defended high); clean untouched levels (A3) stay the preferred targets. Bot (off): `defended_filter`, `defended_band_adr`, `defended_touches`, `defended_bars` (tag defended). Review fix: touches are distinct visits (consecutive in-band bars count once).
- **A4 Bodies vs wicks.** Bodies mark the real level and "tell the story"; wicks "do the damage".
  A 1-2 tick wick beyond a level ("mohawk") still counts as respect (019 ~14:55; 032 ~9:00; 038
  ~46:16; 042 ~1:07:39; 065A ~2:53:30; 096 ~12:04) [2023]. CC: stops sit beyond the body cluster, a
  sweep need not take the wick (cc02_008 ~9:03; cc03_002 ~2:01; cc05_004 ~13:35) [CC]. Bot: a
  sweep = trade past the wick extreme.

**Gaps**
  - Caption-gap fix (A4-wick-poke-vi-invalidation; 090 ~1:05:53: "if it goes back above this high here ... I'm not interested"): after a wick-only poke through a daily / 4h level (no body close beyond) keep the idea and record H = the poke extreme; a trade back beyond H cancels the AM orders of that side; re-evaluate at the 13:30 PM session. Mirror. Bot: `poke_high_cancel` (off; same-side 4h / D arrays of the Confluence object; the PM is not gated). Review fix: "wick-only" = no body since 09:30 closed beyond the array; an already PENDING order of that side is cancelled by the trade beyond H too (`poke_high_cancel`).
  - Caption-gap fix (A4-ltf-body-qualification; 104 ~21:53, ~34:55: "only the candlesticks wicks are piercing it"): a 60m FVG reached by a run is a qualified reversal level when the 15m (also 5m) BODIES stay inside it / do not close beyond its far edge, even if wicks pierce by more than the 1-2 tick mohawk; then expect delivery toward the opposing draw. Bot: `htf_body_qual` (off; the zones of `htf_entry_arrays` count as fresh while the 5m / 15m bodies since they formed hold above the far edge; the turtle-soup external array is NOT covered). Review fix: the same body test lifts the "array not reached since" requirement of the turtle-soup external setup (`htf_body_qual`).
  - Caption-gap fix (A4-sweep-body-cluster; cc03_002 ~11:36: "we only need it to reach below the bodies of the candle", cc02_007 ~21:51): decision `sweep_ref`: "wick" (as built) / "body" (the relative-equal-low pool is a cluster of 2+ candles whose BODIES end at about the same price; the line is the lowest body, a sweep needs a low below it). Daily context: a run below a body-low cluster that is the first return to the zone in ~1.5-2 months (30-45 sessions [our interpretation]) is HTF accumulation - on the LTF ignore the bearish continuation patterns there and buy after a swing high forms and breaks. Bot: `sweep_ref` ("wick"), `daily_first_return` (off; a TAG on the order only - the LTF behaviour change is NOT built), `daily_first_days` (30). Review fix: `daily_first_ltf` builds the LTF half - at the tagged first return the bearish continuation is ignored (the against-side is rejected) and the buy waits for the swing high to break (an MSS-path order).
  - Caption-gap fix (A4-bodies-hold-prev-day-50; 076 ~00:22:29: "look at the bodies respecting 50 of the previous day's range"): one 2023 example (NQ): on a dip into the previous day's range the execution-TF candle bodies hold at / above 50% of the previous day's range while wicks may pierce it; enter on the candle that confirms the reaction inside the IFVG / breaker FVG; a body close under the 50% = a failed confirmation [interpretation]. Mirror. Bot: `pd50_bodies` (off; `pd50_tf` 1 / 5 minutes).
  - Caption-gap fix (A4-event-wick-ignore; cc05_007 ~4:42: "without that Wick we have lower high"): when judging HTF / intermarket swing structure (SMT, correlation), ignore the wick of a candle made by an extraordinary one-off event and use its body extreme. Bot (off): `event_wick_ignore` (a wick beyond the body longer than `event_wick_mult` (2.0) x the 20-session mean range [our interpretation]; the table's daily pivots / untaken swings and the daily SMT use the body extreme).
- **A5 Fair value gap (FVG).** Bullish (BISI) at candle 3: low[c3] > high[c1], zone
  [high c1, low c3]; bearish (SIBI): high[c3] < low[c1]. Known at the close of c3; c2 is the
  displacement candle (001 ~13:02; 024 ~23:22; 056 ~6:08; cc04_012 ~1:52) [both]. Measured on
  wicks (old CC body version cc01_006 is superseded). Bot: `levels.fvgs`, matches.
  - Levels: high, low, CE = 50%, plus 25/75% quadrants on large gaps only (020 ~8:21; 029 ~30:44;
    055 ~4:50; 101 ~51:58) [2023]. CE of a higher-TF FVG is the default session objective (029
    ~44:48).
  - Extend the box to an adjacent volume imbalance; a body gap inside the FVG gives the exact limit
    price (022 ~55:00; 098 ~60:00; cc04_012 ~12:27) [both].
  - Validity: c3 closes, a new candle opens, and price trades back in without first taking the low
    (042 ~34:53). It must sit on the path to the draw (048 ~28:06; 091 ~14:27) [2023].
  - State: a touch = "repriced"; "rebalanced" only after price trades through and leaves; "filled"
    needs bodies through, not wicks (021 ~12:28; 050 ~30:29; cc10_011 ~4:29; cc04_012 ~13:08) [both].
  - Expected reaction: a retrace into a BISI most likely stops at its top or CE; the far edge is
    least likely. When bullish, price should hold the upper quarter (022 ~9:09; 030 ~15:46) [2023].
  - Two FVGs in one leg: the retrace may go to the higher (second) one (042 ~47:20; 082 ~5:31).
    Skip an FVG sitting right above a liquidity pool (060 ~1:10:30) [2023].
  - An FVG overlapping an OB: the FVG near edge comes first (065B ~3:02:40) [2023].
  - Lifetime: extend forward and reuse after fills, up to 60 days back (015 ~22:49; 051 ~19:34; (caption-gap: the 60 days is a W20 / W40 / W60 priority for daily / intraday arrays, weekly-and-higher arrays have no cap - see the fixes below.)
    065B ~3:58:20) [2023].
  - Caption-gap fix (X-efficient-delivery-two-pass; 026 ~1:58:21, 050 ~30:29, 061 ~1:35:24): track `up_pass` / `down_pass` on every gap band (FVG, VI, NDOG, NWOG, wick area, void) on its own TF, wicks counting. No pass = open draw; one pass = one-sided (still a target and S/R, the missing side is the next delivery); both = efficient, not a target; NDOG / NWOG stay as references. A band crossed several times (>= `shield_passes` = 3 [our interpretation]) shields the arrays beyond it. Bot: `two_pass_state` (off; intraday bands, HTF bands are not judged yet).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-caffeine-bar-bisi; 006 ~12:34): a bullish FVG whose c2 is ONE large displacement candle (range >= 2x the mean of the prior 20 bars [our interpretation]) is a significant array; on a normal day any trade below its low (c1 high) kills it; on NFP / FOMC days price can overshoot, so validity is not judged then. Bot: `single_bar_fvg_strict` (off).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-broken-fvg-reclaim; 027 ~22:24, 091 ~20:18): a broken bullish FVG is invalid; it is reclaimed as a bullish discount array only if price first moved away below it (a swing beyond), later came back and closed above its high, and the bias is bullish (not in premium); chop across it does not reclaim (the "or above CE" variant is removed); in bearish / premium context it is an IFVG (A8). Bot: `fvg_reclaim` (off).
  - Caption-gap fix (A11-htf-gap-own-tf; 061 ~40:20; conflict 078 ~1:13:50): an FVG on timeframe T stays inefficient (an open draw) until bodies on T or higher close across it; ignore LTF oscillation inside it when deciding filled / balanced / BPR. 078 lets 4h back-and-forth balance part of a daily gap. Bot: `balance_tf` "own" (default, the spec: only closes of the array's own timeframe retire it) / "legacy" (the earlier build: a weekly array is retired by DAILY closes) / "next_lower" (4h for a daily array, daily for a weekly one).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-vi-extended-ce; 098 ~1:09:26/~1:09:42): when an FVG's forming candles include a VI on the far side - including a wick-only link - extend the zone to the VI's far body edge; CE = the midpoint of (near edge, extended far edge); allow a small overshoot (~2 pts on ES weekly). Bot: `fvg_vi_extend` (the extension) with `fvg_vi_extend_ce` (True: the CE follows the extended zone and a wick-linked VI extends it too - a fix, not a behaviour switch).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-twice-traded-not-fvg; cc05_015 ~11:38): an OB's wick zone that another candle has traded through ("closed in") is not a gap; only one-sided zones count as gaps (A5's strict wick-based FVG already excludes them). Anchor on the OB open (A12-open-vs-wick-gap-criterion, same switch `ob_anchor_rule`).
  - Caption-gap fix (A24-implied-fvg-defined; 035 ~29:49): implied FVG = the range between the CEs (A23) of two wicks, extended forward and used like an FVG; trading through it in the bias direction = strong continuation. [our interpretation] the wicks are c1 and c3 facing a large c2 whose wicks overlap (no true FVG): bullish = c1 upper-wick CE to c3 lower-wick CE. Low confidence (a single mention). Bot: `implied_fvg` (already built, A24.1; `levels.implied_fvgs`).
  - Caption-gap fix (BH-premium-FVG-CE-vs-far-edge-trap; 062 ~15:56: "if it does and it starts to sell off this is a trap"): expected reaction - a retrace into a bearish (premium) FVG that holds at or below the CE and then breaks down into a new SIBI expects the sell-side low to be taken; a retrace to the FVG's FAR (high) edge that then sells off is a trap: the sell side holds and price runs buy-side instead - no short, a neutral zone; mirror for a BISI. Bot: `far_edge_trap` (off; the 60m FVG's far edge = its top - 10% of its height [our interpretation]; a retrace held at or under the CE is tagged `ce_hold`).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-short-into-htf-sibi; 035 ~1:05:16: "I don't want to see it go above the halfway point"): a daily SIBI above price, quartered: the valid short zone is its low up to its CE (best where a 60m / 15m bearish FVG sits in the lowest 25%); enter on the LTF FVG with SMT confirmation (035: index vs DXY; ES / NQ SMT is the substitute [our interpretation]); if price trades above the daily CE before the entry, skip; if already short, a body close above the CE is an exit warning. Bot: `htf_sibi_short_zone` (off; tags `htf_sibi`, `sibi_q1`; exit level `htf_sibi_ce`), `htf_sibi_need_smt` (off). The BISI long is the mirror.
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-premium-half-daily-fvg; 012 ~18:02: "I want to see the majority of the reactions in the upper portion"): inside a large daily BISI, continuation longs come from the upper half; lower-half imbalances should stay open; fills of them or a close below the CE downgrade the setup (expect chop / consolidation, wait). Mirror for a daily SIBI. Bot: `dfvg_half` ("off" / "tag" -> lv_tag `dfvg_lower_half` / "skip" -> reject; the sweep extreme must be at / above the CE less a tick, and no 15m / 1h body may have closed below it today), `dfvg_large_pts` (15, "large" [our interpretation]).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-mtf-gap-refinement; 044 ~45:31, 046 ~19:32, 024 ~21:39): when the entry / target array is an HTF imbalance, look for an LTF FVG of the same displacement inside or overlapping it (any price overlap [our interpretation]) and use its edges / CE as the operative level, keeping the HTF zone as context; for a short the refined gap must still lie beyond the targeted liquidity. A 5m target FVG overlapping a 15m FVG of the same move may extend to the 15m far edge. 024 widens, 044 / 046 refine: `gap_tf_mode` "" / "refine_down" / "widen_up".
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-far-edge-reach-conditions; 058 ~25:40-29:46, 080 ~12:22: "that's layering"): replaces the earlier 'discretionary' remark on whether the far edge is reached. For an HTF FVG entry array drop to a TF ~4-5x lower. (1) Layering: ~3 overlapping back-and-forth swings already printed near the near edge -> the retrace limit is the CE of the most recent small nested LTF FVG. (2) An inner BPR (the gap area already crossed one pass each way) -> no full fill is expected. After (1) or (2) the far edge is protected (a valid stop location) and the gap should stay open. (3) An untraded LTF sub-gap at the far edge makes the far edge a valid entry / target; without it enter at the nested LTF FVG. None of these: the A5 default. Swing count 3 and the TF ratio are [our interpretation]. Bot: `far_edge_reach` (off; HTF entry arrays, tags far_edge_protected / far_edge_layered / far_edge_subgap / far_edge_nested).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-partial-reprice-shrink; 056 ~16:13: "this is only inefficient so it needs only to that level"; cross-reference L9): for a bullish FVG track the part not yet traded through - wicks into the zone from above shrink its top; on the next retrace expect support at the top of the remaining part and rest the limit there; mirror. One example, so a switch: `fvg_edge` "original" / "remaining".
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-skip-fvg-near-stop-pool; 060 ~1:11:00: "this is too close in proximity"): CORRECTED geometry - reject an entry FVG that sits just under a nearby untaken short-term high which itself lies below the protective high (price can run that buy side without reaching the protective high); look for a higher FVG / OB at or above that short-term high instead (a lower TF: 1m / 2m for us). ICT gives no distance threshold [our interpretation: `fvg_pool_gap_pts`, 2 points in the study variant]. The side-space mirror covers both directions. Bot: `fvg_skip_below_pool_short` (off; the pool is a 3-candle swing formed since the sweep and still untaken; the LTF re-pick is the next gap of the same scan).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-multitf-hold-test; 064 ~1:32:01: "the wicks and bodies are not both inside that fair value gap"): for a 5m BISI being retested look at the 1-4m charts: wicks entering the gap while bodies mostly stay above its high = holding, favour the continuation long; bodies closing inside = a weaker hold. Discretionary confirmation; "mostly" = fewer than two closes inside on every chart [our interpretation]. Bot: `mtf_hold` (off; two or more closes inside on any of the 1m-4m charts cancel a 5m entry).
  - Caption-gap fix (A5-ob-in-vi-over-newer-fvg; 009 ~12:07: "I'm looking at the down closed candle"; precedence note for E12): while adding to an open long after the sell side is cleared (the sweep preceded the first fill [our interpretation]) a down-close candle (a bullish OB) that traded into a VI is the add, at that OB's MT, not a later small FVG of the same pullback; the VI below must not be pierced (a touch is allowed) and is the stop reference. This differs from 065B (FVG overlapping an OB -> the FVG near edge first) because the arrays are separate. Bot: `add_pref_ob_in_vi` (off).

- **A6 Gap types** (062 ~43:58) [2023]:
  - Breakaway gap = first FVG leaving a consolidation or expected level after an MSS. It must stay
    at least partly open and may retrace at most to its half; the range it leaves is "protected"
    (001 ~40:03; 002 ~4:35; 003 ~27:46; 062 ~37:02; 065B ~4:39). Fully filled = not breakaway.
  - Measuring gap = FVG near the middle of the move; project swing extreme -> near edge of the gap,
    target -1 SD (003 ~31:35; 011 ~6:37; 061 ~1:22:05; 062 ~42:19).
  - Common gap = ordinary FVG, may be revisited.
  - Caption-gap fix (A6-breakaway-identification; 001 ~42:50, 062 ~34:53, 060 ~1:14:06): a breakaway candidate needs HTF context (price leaves a pre-marked level expected to hold: an HTF FVG edge held by bodies, a BPR, an HTF level respected earlier in the week); no context = common gap. Forms: the first FVG as price accelerates away from the held level (with or without an MSS, 001); the SIBI of the SECOND MSS (the first MSS's FVG is a common gap, 062); the FVG after a blow-off run through the target pool is LIKE a breakaway (060, an analogue). If price returns into it, trade it with the stop beyond the candle bodies; a body close beyond its CE = not breakaway, exit. Bot: `breakaway_def` = "first_after_mss" (default) / "context" (`breakaway_ctx_pts`) / "second_mss".
  - Caption-gap fix (A6-breakaway-depth-qualification; 062 ~37:12/~39:01, 065 ~4:39:02, 075 ~1:39:36, 084 ~29:16, cc04_009 ~13:37): replace "at most half" with the switch `bk_depth`: "ce" (A6: at most 50%) / "any_open" (062, cc04_009: any portion left open, then a move away = breakaway; a full fill = not breakaway). State machine: candidate -> retraced (price trades into the gap, far edge untouched) -> QUALIFIED when price then trades beyond the highest high (lowest low) since the gap formed with the far edge still untouched; only a qualified gap is a protected / trail level. A BPR directly beyond the gap limits the retrace to the near side of the CE (065; past it = downgrade, cancel adds). Conflict: A6 vs 062 vs 065 vs 075 vs cc04_009. Not built: the IFVG-as-breakaway measuring target (084) and the 075 close-into-it variant. Bot: `bk_depth`, `bk_state` (the machine gates trail_breakaway / ladder_trail "breakaway" and tags bk_<state>; a qualified gap that is LATER filled to its far edge / CE is dead again), `bk_bpr_ce`.
  - Caption-gap fix (A6-gap-hierarchy-common; 062 ~39:41/~43:58, cc04_010 ~12:25): for each swing away from an expected HTF level (2 MSSs, 062): breakaway = the gap that leaves the level and stays partly open (trail level); measuring = the gap near the middle that holds on the retrace (projection source); other FVGs = common (may be retested many times and act as S/R after being traded through; bodies at or beyond the edge with wicks piercing = respected). Partials, adds and trails reference breakaway / measuring gaps (062 ~44:40). Naming conflict: 062 "common" = an ordinary FVG, cc04_010 "common gap" = a BODY gap (bearish: open[i] < close[i-1] with no range closing the space; zone [open[i], close[i-1]]; expect bodies to close it) - tag separately. "Never trail behind a common gap" is an extrapolation, a switch. Bot: `gap_classes` (tags gap_<class>, cc_common), `trail_skip_common` (off).
  - Caption-gap fix (A6-voids-fill-holding-range; cc04_002 ~1:41: "liquidity voids will fill in and this is associated with gap risk"): if the current range is not expected to break (consolidating, no displacement beyond its extremes), unfilled voids / FVGs inside it are likely fill targets; a long with a void / FVG between entry and stop or just under the stop gets the stop under the gap (E6) or is skipped. Mirror. "Holding" = the delivery state CONSOLIDATION [our interpretation]. Bot (off): `gap_risk` "widen" | "skip", `gap_risk_near_pts` 2.
  - Caption-gap fix (A6-measuring-gap-anchor; 061 ~01:22:40, 062 ~00:44:32: "project it down to the low of the Gap if we're bearish"): replace "swing extreme -> near edge of the gap" with swing extreme (the root of the energetic leg) -> the gap's FAR edge from the swing (bearish: the SIBI low; bullish: the BISI high); the -1 SD must line up with an old liquidity pool (062). 061 can also be read as duplicating the range from the gap's other edge ("dup"). - **A7 Immediate rebalance** (A7-immediate-rebalance-definition; 059 ~4:16, 060 ~7:29, 061 ~1:23:38, 078 ~1:15:05, 100 ~44:46, 099 ~1:04:01) [2023]; switch `ir_def`:
  - `a7` (first built): the next candle trades straight back to the prior candle's extreme with no gap; a strong continuation signature (048 ~39:02; 060 ~7:20; 092 ~1:41:14; 099 ~3:46). Daily version: the open trades exactly to the prior day's high / low (059 ~4:18).
  - `open_space` (the 059 family; "it doesn't mean it's the previous day immediately to the left"): candle i+1 (or i+2) trades down to the high of the most recent candle that bounds the open space (yesterday's if it overlaps, otherwise the older candle) - within a tick up to a ~1-2 tick overshoot (`ir_overshoot_ticks`), no body close through - then reverses in the bias direction. Stopping short = a breakaway gap (060). [our interpretation] the bounding candle = the most recent earlier candle whose high lies below every low since it.
  - `zero_width` (100): in a displacement leg take c1, c2, c3 with c2 a large bias-direction candle (body >= `ir_body_mult` = 1.5x the mean body of the last 20 bars [our interpretation]); it counts when high[c3] == low[c1] to the tick (bearish) or low[c3] == high[c1] (bullish).
  - Both modes: need a known draw in that direction (the bias); tag `fast delivery expected` (`ir_fast_<mode>`); the level is a PD array on that TF; with bias, enter or add with a tight stop beyond c3's extreme; first partial at the nearest opposing pool; exit on a retrace back through the range EQ (X8). Conflict: A7 (yesterday, exact, next candle) vs the 059 family vs 100. Bot: `ir_def` ("a7" default), `ir_overshoot_ticks`, `ir_body_mult`; `ir_entries` (M11a) enters at once.
  - Caption-gap fix (A7-immediate-rebalance-gates; 059 ~4:50, 060 ~7:29, 080 ~9:41: "the immediate rebalance isn't going to help you all that much"): an immediate rebalance is a continuation trigger only if (a) the draw / HTF bias was known beforehand and points its way and (b) it prints in a window where volatility is expected ([our interpretation] the macro window or an am / pm session); otherwise do not act on it (also M11). Review fix: the window is judged by the clock (macro, am/pm sessions, Silver Bullet windows), not by the session label.
- **A8 Inversion FVG (IFVG)** = an FVG that a candle closes beyond; it then acts in the opposite
  role (015 ~21:00; 061 ~19:42; 078 ~53:07; 102 ~1:02:03) [2023].
  - Valid only when a candle closes beyond, a NEW candle opens, trades back into it and it holds; a
    spike through and back does not count (048 ~21:32, ~36:40).
  - Allowed depth: one prior pass -> may reach the far edge; partly re-traded -> near edge to CE;
    traded both ways -> near edge, CE at most (102 ~1:09:00). Bearish IFVG retraces usually stop at
    CE or a little above (078 ~50:28).
  - Bias switch: the same 1m gap is a long target before the HTF array is hit and an IFVG short
    after it (086 ~14:12).

An OB closed below: expect resistance at its low and MT, shorts once displacement lower follows; a body close back through the MT invalidates the new role. Bot: `inversions` (empty; subset of ob / breaker / vi / org / nwog) = entry arrays (support role) and targets (resistance role).

- **A9 Volume imbalance (VI)** = consecutive bodies do not overlap while wicks do; bullish:
  min(o,c)[i] > max(o,c)[i-1] and low[i] <= high[i-1] (003; 009 ~2:16; 022 ~54:26; 090 ~13:01)
  [2023]. Use its low as a stop; it may be touched but not pierced (009 ~13:46). It is dead once a
  later body on its own (or higher) TF closes fully across it (035 ~5:50; 090 ~13:01). VIs are

Balanced once a later bar's range lies fully beyond Z on the far side [our interpretation]; a bullish BPR below price is support, a bearish one above is resistance; a long's stop may sit below its low; a body close below EQ or the low = idea wrong. - **A13 Breaker.** Bullish: swing low L1, swing high H, lower low L2 that runs L1's sell stops, then
  price breaks above H; the zone is the up-close candle at H. Bearish mirror uses the down-close
  candle at the swing low (001 ~45:02; 007 ~22:46; 012 ~11:07; cc04_005 ~0:37-3:32) [both].
  - Candle: the highest up-close candle in the swing by body (029 ~24:13; 048 ~1:06:07; 096 ~6:42).
    Bot: last up-close candle within `pivot_n` bars before H.
  - Zone extent (body, full range, A-B leg, last candle): D6 (`breaker_zone` range default /
    body / leg / last). MT of the zone should not be closed through (018
    ~1:22; 070 ~56:01).
  - First touch is valid; bodies respect, wicks may poke (022 ~2:47:00; 033 ~57:56).
  - A high-low-higher-high at the wrong time of day is "time distortion", not a breaker (070 ~6:24).

- **A15 Mitigation block** = the breaker's failure-swing twin: high A, swing low, LOWER high B (no
  stop run above A), break below the swing low; the zone is the last down-close candle in that

- **A22 PD-array matrix**, outward from EQ: mitigation block, breaker, liquidity void, FVG, OB,
  rejection block, old high/low; discount mirror (cc05_014 ~18:20-29:36; cc07_004 ~1:01) [CC].
  Price meets them nearest first; a breaker in the path usually stops price before the arrays
  beyond it (cc05_014 ~21:04; cc09_008 ~9:59). Skip exhausted arrays, prefer untested (cc07_004
  ~4:00).
  - Caption-gap fix (A22-htf-definition-nested-arrays; cc10_013 ~8:17 / ~13:44, cc10_014 ~11:08: "nested ideas of premium array would be our catalyst"): a NESTED array = a 15 / 60m PD array overlapping a 4h or daily array of the same polarity. PM reversal: if the AM move stopped at a nested (HTF) array expect the PM to fade it; if at an un-nested 15 / 60m array expect continuation. HTF set per filter: reversal {4h, D, W+}; consolidation profiles {1h, 4h+}. Bot: `pm_rev_nested` (off; any price overlap [our interpretation]; blocks `pm_reversal` without the nested array and without a Confluence object). Review fix: the reversal set is {4h, D, W}; an AM extreme inside an HTF array counts as nested, one only inside an un-nested 15 / 60m array blocks the fade.

  ~16:43) [2023]. A long wick in the bias direction often stops dead at its CE; a next-candle close
  beyond the CE = continuation, close back = revisit (025 ~25:37; 100 ~38:13). Bot:

Quarters / gradients: q25 (25% from the body edge), CE, q75. A retrace into an upper wick used as resistance: turns at q25 = very bearish; at CE = normal; overshoots CE but stays <= q75 = still valid (103); trades through q75 or a body closes above CE = failed [our tiers]. Heavy bearish stance: q25 is the reaction / entry level (025 / 028). Bias line (065 / 075, lower wick 1h+): bullish intact while no body closes at or below CE; a body close at or below it = stop adding (judged only once that 1h candle has closed). ### 2.2 Bias (higher timeframe)

- **B1 Why price moves.** Price moves only to run liquidity (old or relative equal highs/lows) or to
  rebalance an inefficiency (FVG, VI, gap, wick, void, up to ~60 days old); otherwise it
  consolidates (038 ~47:04; 050 ~8:01; 061 ~41:29; 104 ~14:19; 065A ~46:30) [2023].
  - Caption-gap fix (B1-nearby-unrun-high-trap; cc04_013 ~6:11): do not short a signal forming within X of an untaken old high / REH above - expect the pool to be run first (mirror for longs). X is not given: our proxy 0.25 x ADR5. Bot (off): `near_pool_block`, `near_pool_adr`.
  - Caption-gap fix (BH-B1-imbalance-ceiling-ladder; 011 ~00:00:24: "as long as we remain below that all the discount arrays ... would be reached") / L14: an overhead daily VI / FVG is a ceiling: while no body closes above it the lower arrays (REL, FVG, OB, then the sell-side) are drawn in that order; suspended once price traded above it and the retrace held it as support (two imbalances supporting). Mirror. Bot (off): `ceiling_ladder` (table `ceiling` / `ladder_dn`, `floor` / `ladder_up`; the lock follows the ladder).
  - Caption-gap fix (E4-daily-one-way-delivery-rebalance; cc03_007 ~23:41: "price has to be efficiently balanced"): a daily leg delivered one way (consecutive down-close candles after the last up-close candle U) is an inefficiency: expect price to rebalance it by trading back up through the leg into U's range (U = the draw / target); mirror for an up-only leg; a draw / target tag (single example), not an entry trigger. Bot (off): `oneway_n` (>= n down-close days after U make U's range a table draw; no reaction weight).
  - Caption-gap fix (B1-eq-consolidation-breakout; cc06_002 ~7:55: "if price is at equilibrium we're going to be looking for an opportunity to see that price move to an imbalance"): swing corollary - while price sits at the EQ inside a consolidation take no swing position; once it breaks out (a daily close beyond the range [our interpretation]) it travels to the nearest untraded weekly / monthly imbalance in that direction (a premium array up, a deep-discount array down). Bot (off): `eq_breakout_draw` (want = the breakout side, 0 at the EQ; the imbalance is the draw).
- **B2 Draw search order:** inefficiency first; if none, liquidity: from premium, sell-side below an
  old low; from discount, buy-side above a short-term high (065A ~50:00). Beyond the obvious pool,
  the nearest inefficiency sets how far the run goes (104 ~16:18) [2023].

- **B23 Seasonals** (road map, never a trigger; use only when long- and short-sample lines agree and
  HTF trend agrees; a missing seasonal signals strength the other way) (cc05_010; cc05_011; 074
  ~49:22) [both]. Index map (Dow 20-yr, cc10_015 ~5:04): Jan bearish, Feb bullish, Mar
  consolidation, Apr bullish, May bearish, Jun consolidation ending bearish, Jul bullish, Aug
  consolidation, Sep split, Oct-Dec bullish; Mar/Jun/Aug low probability. Sources disagree on
  January and late May (cc10_016; 050 ~2:20; 074 ~16:54). Bot: tag only.
  - Caption-gap fix (B23-index-seasonal-windows / B23-intramonth-detail / B23-seasonal-quality / B23-daily-seasonal-review; 050 ~2:22; 074 ~18:21; 079 ~24:47; 100 ~29:55; 102 ~10:24; cc10_015 ~2:03-12:40; cc10_016-018; cc05_012 ~13:05): the regime layer (Jan-Apr high-magnitude and mostly bullish; May-Oct low-magnitude range; Q4 strongly bullish) and the windows - long: mid-Sep/Oct to year end, extending to about the first week of Feb, and Feb to May; short: May into the 1st-2nd week of June (extendable through July to about Aug 1 or mid-Sep), and January; in bull regimes the 3rd week of May to the 3rd week of June is a buy window (the June low); start preparing about a month before a window. Conflicts to flag: January bearish vs the fall long window into early Feb; July bullish into the mid-year high vs the May-July short window; the May-June short vs the June-low buy. Intramonth (Dow/index): Sep first half bullish, second half bearish; Nov bullish, Dec Santa Claus; Jul bullish into the mid-year high, Aug consolidation; Mar consolidation (2nd-3rd week bearish, a rally into month end); Apr opens slightly bearish then rallies from mid-month; May bearish into the 3rd week then a small rally. Delivery quality (a SIZE / participation filter, not a direction): prime = Sep 1 (079) or Oct 1 (cc10_015) to about Dec 10, especially Oct 1-Nov 15, plus Feb 1-May 31; poor = August and Dec 11-31; everything else off-season; May overlaps with 'May-Oct less directional'; March is low probability in B23 but sits in the spring prime window (test both). The Dec wind-down starts about the second week of December (102). Each date also carries the month's seasonal direction and the next month's. Bot: `season_windows` "tag" (regime_*, win_*, season_intra_*, season_result_*), `season_quality` "tag" / "size", `seasonal_tag` (season_dir_*, season_next_*); the report breaks out `by_dw_tag`.

A window that passes with no setup is read: price going the other way = strength there (the bias flips); little net movement = consolidation; several asset classes off their seasonals = decoupling, no strong one-sided moves. Add-ons: for a pair the trend in either leg is enough (FX; for index futures the instrument's own HTF trend); a suspected fresh long-term turn may be tested with a trade (no smaller size given); a bearish window gives a large decline only when the weekly tone and institutional order flow are already bearish (a bullish weekly tone: only a minor dip); prime = bearish months in bear markets, bullish in bull markets, and the underlying trend outweighs the seasonal; take only windows whose composite shows a sizable swing - be selective (no numeric threshold from ICT; the cut is an owner choice); the HTF regime picks the window (an opposing window is skipped or is the expected pullback into the next window's low); a dominant macro regime (2008) overrides seasonals; a consolidation through a bearish window that then breaks up means the window failed (mirror). Bot: `season_gate` (intramonth direction; the window acts only when the weekly tone agrees [season_win_prime, else season_win_minor], the 252-session trend agrees [else season_pullback], its composite ranks at least `season_min_rank` from PRIOR years only, and no flagged macro direction opposes it; a failed window blocks the other side), `season_move_adr` (the counter / consolidation yardstick), `macro_risk_dates`.

One source, judgment; the nearest existing match is the M-model "three drives into failing liquidity". - **B25 Three pools run = exhaustion** (BH-three-pool-runs-exhaustion; 048 ~1:13:41, DXY: "it's already taken three pools of liquidity"): once a leg has taken three successive pools in the order's direction (a short: sell-side pools), do not take new continuation orders at premium arrays (FVG / OB) in that leg - expect consolidation or a run to the nearest opposite pool; the mirror for buy-side runs. The source example is DXY; applying it to the indices is [our interpretation]. Bot: `pool_runs_max` (0 = off; test 3, `sm2.pool_runs`, reject `x_pool_runs_exhausted`).
- **B26 No HTF top / bottom picking** (BH-no-HTF-top-picking; 061 ~01:40:32; 074 ~00:37:14; 076 ~00:05:21; cc06_001 ~00:06:55; 104 ~00:44:07: "we do not try to pick tops in buy programs okay and we don't try to pick bottoms and sell programs") [both]: do not pick HTF tops in buy programs or HTF bottoms in sell programs, however overbought or oversold price looks; stay with the HTF bias until the market clearly shows a reversal, then trade the new bias. LTF tops and bottoms in the HTF direction are allowed. A smart money reversal (selling within 1-2 ticks of an extreme) is for bearish markets only and for experts, never in bull markets; bottoms mirror. Bot mapping (ours, off): `no_htf_top_pick` (with the flip state up and the draw unmet, no turtle-soup / three-drives / reversal shorts; mirror).
- **B27 Minimum criteria for a high-probability directional bias** (BH-B4-HTF-bias-minimum-criteria; 075 ~00:34:50: "we have now met the minimum criteria for high probability directional bias") [2023]: a 1h bullish bias is met when, in order, price reaches the HTF bullish array (a daily propulsion block), sweeps 1h relative equal lows (sell-side) and trades above the most recent 1h short-term high (the MSS, no close needed); then retracements into the array's mean threshold are buys while the candle bodies respect it. Mirror. Not part of B4. Bot (off): `min_criteria` (the lock is neutral until the three steps are met; entries need bodies to stay beyond the array MT).
- **B28 A run, then consolidation: the run's extreme is the minimum draw** (BH-consolidation-takes-run-high; 046 ~00:09:16: "at the very minimum take out the old high") [both]: a tendency, not a rule: after a price run followed by consolidation expect the run's high to be taken at minimum (usually), whether price continues or reverses via a breaker; a tie-breaker lean when the bias is unclear. Timeframe unspecified (4h / daily ranges): a leg of >= 2 x ADR5 followed by >= 2 inside days [ours]. Bot (off): `run_extreme_lean`.
- **B29 The PWH run and the give-back** (BH-PWH-run-giveback-weekly-gap; 047 ~00:01:31: "we've given up much of the ground gained over the week so far") [index]: tentative tag: after the week runs above the previous week's high and gives back much of the week's up-move (> 50 % [ours]), consider the nearest weekly BISI (its CE, then the full gap) and the sell-side below as the next draw; a full close into that gap = lean bearish, lower targets (the `wk_bisi` trigger). Mirror. CONFLICT with B17 (a delivered draw = neutral). Bot (off): `pwh_giveback_draw` (+ `giveback_frac`).

- **B30 Monthly structure control** (new-monthly-structure-control; cc12_001 ~23:53: "we've probably made an intermediate term or long-term high"): on the monthly chart, higher highs then a lower high and a further lower high = a long-term high is in control (shorts); low, higher low, higher low = a long-term low in control (longs); confirm with SMT (FX: DXY; indices SPX / NDX / DJI - our adaptation; the SMT part is not built). Bot (off): `monthly_ctrl` (table m_ctrl from levels.swing_tiers on the monthly bars; a neutral bias takes it). `monthly_ctrl_smt` confirms it with the monthly SMT of the two index frames (one index fails the higher monthly high / lower monthly low within the last 3 completed months [our interpretation]); without a partner frame the gate is inert.
### 2.3 Day selection and news

*Filters first (OT-filters-before-bias; cc08_006 ~19:14: "regardless of what we have as a daily bias ... all those things fall second to this criteria"): any no-trade filter (N1-N10 and the CC day filters) vetoes the session before bias is read; a strong or ideal bias never overrides it. Bot: `dwc3.session_veto` is that first step (the calendar block, then `skip_news`) in the main loop, the Silver Bullet and Ma Deuce; the per-bar sloppy stand-aside is logged as a veto row too; every veto is appended to the run's skips. PARTIAL: the side blocks (`block_side`) and the day-level `want` skip stay where they were (owner question).*

- **N6 News delivery profile**: the side taken first after the release is usually false; the
  opposite pool is the real move (up-down-up / down-up-down) (064 ~52:12; 073 ~10:42; 078
  ~1:25:47) [2023]. After both pools are taken, price retraces to the CE between them (064
  ~1:10:43). On NFP/CPI/FOMC days breakers reject poorly (073 ~4:36).
  - Caption-gap fix (N6-nfp-first-move-fake-if-liquidity; 105 ~7:58 (also ~2:00-2:30): "I don't see this initial rally as the fake move"): the first post-release move is false ONLY if it ran a defined pool (an old high / low or relative equal highs / lows). If it takes no pool on its side, the counter move that follows is the Judas - e.g. a drop into an HTF PD array such as a 4h FVG in discount, aligned with the bias: enter in the bias direction at that array and target the pools the first move left untaken. Pool-penetration tolerance = `sweep_ticks` [our interpretation]. Bot: `news_profile="pool_test"` (off; a pool taken = as "require"; otherwise orders in the counter-move's direction are blocked until price is at a 1h / 4h array of their side, and only the bias-direction entry at the array is the trade, `x_news_pool_bias`; the pools move 1 left untaken on its own side are that entry's targets).
  - Caption-gap fix (N6-both-pools-news; 064 ~30:16: "Both sides of the marketplace are always in contention for high impact news drivers"): before 08:30 on CPI / NFP / FOMC days record the nearest smooth REH, REL and the nearest unfilled FVG; expect both pools to be taken after the release, often the FVG first (overshoot allowed), possibly within the first 1m candle; observation only (N4/N5); then N6: the first pool is usually false, a retrace to the CE between the pools follows. Non-news days play the same sequence more slowly (064 ~1:07:20). Bot (study, off): `news_seq_study` (order tags `news_seq_*`, `news_sequence.csv`).
  - Caption-gap fix (N6-news-runs-trailed-stops; 065 ~4:11:02): in an established trend the release move runs first against the trend into the trend-holders' trailed stops or the pool beneath them, then usually resumes the trend; hold no position into the release; decide reversal vs resumption only from how price delivers. Bot (off): `news_trend_side` (after the news range only the daily-bias side, and only when the range's counter-trend extreme swept a pool).
  - Caption-gap fix (N6-news-pool-mapping; 037 ~11:55: "the highs that haven't been returned to"): a study exercise on days he would not trade (NFP, FOMC, chair testimony): before the event annotate the 5m / 15m untaken highs and the sell-side lows near price, then watch which pool the event takes first and whether the other follows; his speculation (not a rule): the opposite pool first, then the approached pool, then the FVG beyond it. Observation only: `study.news_pool_rows` (`--news-pools`).
- **N7 Holidays and calendar** [both]:
  - Day before a long weekend/holiday (and bank holidays): skip (cc08_006 ~5:06; cc10_008 ~6:54;
    cc10_019 ~15:02) [CC].
  - Morning after a holiday: choppy, no trade; PM or next day once ES/NQ move in step (090 ~8:00)
    [2023]. After a Monday holiday, Tuesday acts like Monday, Wednesday is the likely reversal day
    (cc09_008 ~55:24).
  - A holiday early in NFP week (Tuesday July 4) delivers cleaner Thu/Fri (072 ~3:36); other holiday weeks are wonky (045 ~44:04) [2023].
  - Last trading day of the year: low volatility, expect an inside day; first trading day: don't
    rush (Ep 01; Ep 02).
  - Futures rollover week: more volatile and choppy (093 ~0:32). Bot: `roll_week` tag (calendar, the data's roll flag, or either: `roll_week_src`), `roll_risk`, `skip_roll_week` (N7.5).
  - August: choppy "meat grinder", trade small or stand aside, the whole month (079

- **N8 Day type** [both]:
  - Consolidation = no trade; wait for expansion (cc01_001; cc09_003).
  - Trending-day recipe needs all three: small/contracting prior ranges, price just traded into a
    daily PD array, and high/medium 08:30 news (cc10_009 ~1:17-2:46) [CC].
  - Consolidation-day predictors: no NY report today with reports later in the week; price at an
    HTF array or HTF 50%; holiday eve; auction day (cc10_008 ~1:51-7:47; 053 ~0:38). On such days:
    AM scalps only if the opening range is small, done by 11:00, PM only if the AM has not run a
    stop pool (cc10_008 ~9:01-17:19) [CC].
  - Large opening range gap or very large prior-day range: the first 30-45 min chop; sacrifice the
    AM, trade 13:30-16:00 (033 ~13:27) [2023].
  - Prior day's range > 2x the 5-day ADR (ADR5): skip, chop (cc08_006 ~1:22, London); prior day
    under ADR5: expansion of 1.5-2x due (cc08_006 ~23:33) [CC].
  - "Seek & Destroy" (both sides run, then reversal): don't hold trades; such weeks come while
    waiting on rates/NFP and in July-August (024 ~13:57; 044 ~1:36:11; cc07_002 ~7:42).
  - Caption-gap fix (N8-seek-destroy-week; cc07_002 ~7:57; cc07_003 ~33:45: "it's probably better for you to sit on your hands and just don't trade it"): the week is Seek & Destroy when Mon-Wed/Thu consolidates and runs both the running weekly high and low by a small amount (no daily close beyond them [ours]) AND FOMC or NFP is pending this week, or the month is July or August. Then take no trades through the expansion, Friday's included: watch Thursday's high/medium news to raid one side, then a run beyond all intra-week extremes to a daily or weekly PD array; only after that array is hit look for a reversal, which needs a new setup (cc07_002 calls the Friday leg an expansion, cc07_003 expects a reversal after the array). Bot: `sd_week_detect` (with `sd_week_skip`), `sd_shallow_frac`, `sd_after_array`.
  - Caption-gap fix (N8-intraday-seek-destroy; 015 ~21:31; 024 ~19:27: "That is a highly manipulated market environment."): flag the session 'manipulated / seek_and_destroy' if early on (09:30-10:30 [ours]) price takes a short-term high's buy stops and a short-term low's sell stops in turn, in a narrow range with no displacement. Then: no 1m entries inside the range; no entry until an MSS leaves an FVG that a later retrace holds; any trade exits fully at the first target (no runner, no trailing); after price reaches the pre-marked HTF level, no trade on the reversal off it; with no confirmed MSS expect chop all day. Bot: `sd_intraday` "tag" / "rules" (single exit, no entry before the range breaks by a close, none after the locked level traded; `sd_fvg_hold` (default on): after the close beyond the range wait for the first FVG in that direction and a retrace into it that closes without a body through it).

  - Caption-gap fix (N8-overnight-not-precursor; cc10_009 ~0:55: "it's never a precursor"): overnight price action, trending or ranging, is not a precursor of a trending day - the trending-day / consolidation classification leaves overnight features out (`n8_use_overnight` False; conflicts with N9's 2023 narrow-overnight rule, which stays its own switch `min_on_range_frac`).
  - Caption-gap fix (N8-consolidation-builds-orders; cc01_002 ~17:28): the consolidation range edges are the pools for the coming expansion. While consolidating: no entries in skip mode; mark the range high / low as buy-side / sell-side pools (L1); the next leg is an impulsive expansion out of the range, never a retracement or reversal straight out of it; afterwards expect a retracement to the OB it left (then continuation) or a reversal; take the direction from the HTF premise (a false run of the opposite extreme is the manipulation); trade only after the expansion has printed. Bullish mirror: a short-term low just under the EQ broken on an expansion away from it is a buy. Bot: `consol_edge_fade` "inner" / "both" (off; `consol_eq_band` 0.15 of the range height). - **N9 In-day no-trade conditions** [2023]:
  - No clear draw, no inefficiency, no displacement: no trade (045 ~31:22-40:49).
  - Draw delivered before 09:30: choppy 09:30, trade the PM (022 ~1:16:11).
  - Daily objective done before lunch: skip the rest of the day (015 ~16:22).
  - Caption-gap fix (N9-objective-before-lunch; 015 ~16:22: "it had already delivered the lion's portion of the move"): CORRECTION - if the move toward the daily objective is mostly delivered (more than about half [ours]) before the planned entry, take no continuation entry toward it; once the objective is reached (before or at lunch), PM retracement/reversal setups (the 2:00 macro, M6) targeting the lunch-hour lows (bullish day) or highs are allowed - the rest of the day is not skipped. Bot: `objective_frac_gate`, `objective_done_pm` "reversal_only" (`objective_done_skip` keeps the old whole-PM skip).
  - Overnight range too narrow, or the pre-09:30 raid too shallow: no AM trade (015 ~11:58).
  - Macro window (09:50-10:10 or 10:50-11:10) produces no move toward the draw: close the charts
    for that session (073 ~30:51). Applies to every macro in use, not only the two AM ones; failure = the market does not budge toward the draw; it ends the session, maybe the day (073 ~30:51, ~35:33; `macro_fail_windows`, `macro_fail_scope`).
  - London already made ~80% of ADR5: skip NY (cc08_001 ~13:41) [CC].
  - Caption-gap fix (DS2-ny-continuation-adr-pending; cc09_003 ~12:11; cc09_006 ~18:28) [CC, FX-derived; test as an index filter]: NY continuation long (entries 07:00-10:00) needs HTF bias bullish, London (00:00-05:00) posted the day low with displacement, no 4h / daily premium array touched intraday, and today's range so far < ADR5; entry: buy a run below a 5m short-term low into a discount array; targets: the ADR5 high (LOD + ADR5 [ours]) and the next 60m / 4h premium array; if ADR5 is reached before 10:00 take 80 % and leave a runner (L16). If ADR5 is already met or an HTF premium array was hit: no NY continuation (`ny_cont_gate`). cc08_001 ~13:41 skips NY at ~80 % of ADR5, these sources use 100 %.
  - Caption-gap fix (DS2-adr-filled-news-extends; cc09_007 ~18:32: "the average daily range will likely be exceeded especially if high impact news is due out after equities open") [CC, FX source]: if the day range is already >= ADR5 at or before the NY open AND a high-impact release is scheduled after 09:30 (10:00, 11:00 or afternoon), expect ADR5 to be exceeded (not doubled): the 80 % London skip above does not apply (`adr_news_exempt`, tag `adr_filled_news`); with no later news the skip stands. Single source, partly discretionary: `london_low_guard` (tick-through rule; the "15m bodies, wicks allowed" wording is covered by the stricter rule).

ICT: not an invitation for new traders. Bot (off): `pm_narrative`, `pm_narr_gap_pts` = 5 [our interpretation].
  - Caption-gap fix (N9-ny-continues-london; cc09_007 ~00:07:58: "unless a higher time frame discount array has been hit"): if London (02:00-05:00) makes the high of the day with bearish IOF, expect the NY session to continue lower and short premium arrays in the NY kill zone, unless an HTF discount array was already hit intraday and / or the ADR5 low was already reached; targets: the ADR5 low and the next 4h / 60m discount array; mirror for longs. - **N10 Day of week** [both]:
  - Sunday open: don't trade it (011 ~4:20; 028 ~1:16:09; 103).
  - Monday: new students don't trade it (065B ~4:12); outside NFP/FOMC weeks he skips it unless
    obvious, or trades the PM only (101 ~30:53); "let Monday go", enter Tue off Monday's low or
    Wed off Tuesday's low (cc12_003 ~15:40). Many 2023 examples are Mondays (007, 018/019, 036,
    053, 069). D20.
  - Tue and Wed are ideal; Thu good but can reverse and often makes the opposite end of the week
    (cc08_001 ~18:52; 044 ~16:25; 060 ~1:11:50; 099 ~1:02:09). The weekly high/low forms Mon-Wed
    ~70% of the time (cc07_008 ~1:35) [CC]; a Thu/Fri break of the Mon-Wed extreme = expansion to
    target (cc07_001 ~42:44).
  - Friday: small range unless the weekly objective is unmet (cc08_001); take most at the first pool
    because the HTF target may not come (070 ~1:06:37); no trade after a week whose targets were all
    met (100 ~7:05); don't buy Friday after a strong Thursday (cc09_008 ~41:53). TGIF: M14.
  - No positions over the weekend or across 17:00-18:00 (065A ~2:30:30; 101 ~4:27; 103 ~9:31).
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-thursday-caps-week; cc08_001 ~21:59): the weekly range is usually capped by Thursday's NY session; a Thursday London trade in the weekly direction may fizzle and the week may reverse in Thursday NY or the London close (10:00-12:00). Handling [our interpretation]: bank Thursday pre-09:30 positions by 10:00; a Thursday NY-morning new weekly extreme into a daily PD array is a candidate weekly high / low - no further entries in its direction that day. Bot: `thu_cap` (off; filter `x_thu_week_ext`, exit `thu_cap`).
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-friday-preopen-delivered; 035 ~39:24: "more likely for the market to enter a consolidation or retrace"): on a Friday, if most of the expected move was delivered before 09:30, the index is at its intraday / HTF objective, an SMT failure prints there (DXY fails to confirm the extreme) and NY lunch is near: no new continuation entries and no PM trade toward a further objective; expect consolidation or a retrace into the close (`fri_delivered_stop`; "most of the move" = the locked draw delivered, SMT against the partner index, [ours]). Conflict with N9 (022): a draw delivered pre-09:30 sends you to the PM.
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-friday-pool-choice; 096 ~6:54; cc04_011 ~18:05: "because it's Friday it's likely to see it run up against buy stops"): on a Friday, price that has already run a nearby sell-side pool and sits under unrun buy-side (session or overnight high) favours a long toward the buy-side; do not use the remaining REL below as the draw (`fri_pool_choice`; mirror; targets capped at the week's extreme). After a one-way Mon-Thu expect a choppy Friday: nearest pools in the trend direction only, no runner, no new-extreme target; afterwards a run of the opposite side. Overlap with M14 TGIF (20-30 % retrace after a trending week vs cc04_011's choppy run of nearby buy stops, FX / DXY): discretionary context, tag it.
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-monday-weekly-extreme; cc07_008 ~29:09; cc08_001 ~20:18, ~33:18: "if Monday is a big range day and we trade up into a premium relative to the Daily then we can really anticipate that being the high of the week"): bearish week: the weekly-high candidates are Mon, Tue, Wed in order; assume Monday is the high and sell only a small piece above it; Mondays are normally small range. Exception: Monday (or the Sunday-open-to-Frankfurt range at 00:00 NY, an FX context) is a large-range move into a daily premium PD array: anticipate Monday as the weekly high and watch Monday London (`monday_mode="big_into_array"`, `mon_big_frac` [ours]; weekly-bias side only at half size; tag `wk_ext_candidate`). Conflict with N10 / cc12_003 "let Monday go" (D20).
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-mon-wed-range-break; cc07_001 ~44:15: "if that is broken on an intra-week basis that is confirmation that you are an aggressive sell-off"): the Mon-Wed range runs from Monday's open (optionally Sunday's) through Wednesday; with a bullish HTF bias a Thu / Fri break above its high confirms the buy program (aggressive expansion to the predefined weekly / monthly premium array); bearish: a break below the low. Only the bias-side break counts (`mw_break_bias`); a target not reached that week keeps the same bias for the next week (`mw_carry`). Hold or add with the bias; no counter-bias trades [ours].
  - Caption-gap fix (DS2-monday-ideal-day; cc08_007 ~7:17, ~21:02; cc09_007 ~22:37; cc09_008 ~55:20; cc12_004 ~6:30: "the ideal days of the week buying are Monday Tuesday and Wednesday"): the primary day-trade days are Mon, Tue and Wed in the HTF / daily order-flow direction; Thu / Fri use the late-week templates only when the Mon-Wed move fails (`days_mode="mon_wed"`; D20 option B). Conflict: cc12_003 ~15:40 "let Monday go", 065B ~4:12 and 101 ~30:53 against these four CC lessons.
  - Caption-gap fix (DS2-mon-wed-fallback-thu-fri; cc12_004 ~6:30: "then I'm going to be focusing on Thursday and Friday templates"): with the HTF bias set before the week (Sunday open checked for a gap) expect the bias-direction move Mon-Wed; if it does not materialize (or the bias was wrong), switch to the late-week (Thu / Fri) templates and weekly profiles, chosen with that week's calendar (`late_week_fallback`; delivered = the week moved >= 1 x ADR5 with the bias by Wednesday's close [ours]). Conflicts with N2's "nothing by Wed 11:00 = week done" in NFP weeks.
  - Caption-gap fix (DS2-tue-to-thu-horizon; cc12_003 ~15:56: "if I can get Tuesday's action into Thursday's New York open then I got it"): swing within the week: skip the Monday entry; Tuesday: an OTE retrace referenced to Monday's low [ours: 62-79 % of the Monday-low-to-swing-high leg, stop below Monday's low]; if Tuesday does not fill, Wednesday off Tuesday's low; the aim is the Tuesday-to-Thursday leg, held into Thursday's NY open (08:30-11:00 [ours]); bearish mirror (`tue_off_monday="ote"`; the horizon is a study field). Complements "let Monday go", no contradiction.
  - Caption-gap fix (DS2-weekly-template-sequence; cc09_008 ~41:53; cc12_003 ~14:19, ~8:45): bullish week: the weekly low forms Mon / Tue / Wed (Wednesday below Tuesday's low into the buy level is the expected weekly low); expansion Tuesday's second half through Wednesday into Thursday's first half; longs allowed until Thursday's NY open, the weekly high expected by ~Thursday noon, no new longs after; Friday is a retracement (no longs if Thursday closed higher) - `no_long_after_thu_noon` (with N10.4 `no_fri_after_strong_thu`). Bearish week: the weekly high forms Tuesday, shorts Tue-Wed, the weekly low at or around Thursday's NY open, Monday and Friday consolidation (the bullish template allows a Monday low). Default assumptions, not certainties.
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-unmet-target-carries-to-monday; 061 ~2:32:51: "they're going to leave that portion open and trade it in Monday") N10.6: a weekly draw unmet at Friday's close keeps its bias and target for Monday / Tuesday; no counter-bias trades until it hits, then neutral for the rest of the week (Tuesday to Friday, from the completed sessions). Weekend holds stay banned. Bot (off): `carry_weekly_draw`, `carry_neutral_after` (on).
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-thursday-reversal-after-targets-met; 099 ~1:02:20; 100 ~8:24; cc07_007 ~22:51) N10.3: Thursday reversal applies after the week's HTF targets were met Mon-Wed - treat it as a retracement, do not press with-trend, keep the HTF bias until B8 flips; base rate ~1 Wed / Thu reversal week a month. Bot (off): `thu_targets_met_block`.
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-friday-skip-profitable-week; 100 ~7:18: "if you have profits on the week the worst thing you could possibly do is offer them up on the altar") N10.4: skip Friday when the week is profitable, not only when the weekly targets are met. Bot (off): `skip_fri_if_week_up`.
  - Caption-gap fix (N10-mon-wed-extreme-trending-week; cc07_006 ~21:27) N10.3: the ~70% Mon-Wed extreme applies to trending weeks and forms at an HTF discount (premium) array, incl. the 20 / 40 / 60-day range levels. Bot (off): `mw_extreme_need_array` (+ `trend_k` = 0.8 [our interpretation of trending]); the weekday of the weekly extreme is tagged Thu / Fri.

Bot (off): `missed_week_mode` "nearest" | "skip".

### 2.4 Times and sessions

  - Caption-gap fix (BH-N10-thursday-not-top-call; 044 ~16:07, ~1:21:30: "I'm not calling the top"): Thursday after a one-sided, algorithmically delivered week: 'the opposite end of the week' is an expectation, not a top call; a counter-trend entry needs a stricter-than-normal reversal - a sweep at an HTF premium array, a clear displaced MSS with an FVG, preferably in the 10:00 hour (mirror for down weeks). `thu_strict`.
  - Caption-gap fix (D20-landmine-week-monday; 064 ~10:18: "This week is littered with landmines ... you want to try to get yourself up on Monday"): a week with two or more high-impact releases (CPI, FOMC, NFP ...) on Tue-Fri makes Monday a trading day, exempt from skip_monday (extends the NFP/FOMC exemption; N4/N5 still apply before each release; no skip-rest-of-week clause from this source; new students still do not trade Monday, N10). `landmine_min`.
  - Caption-gap fix (NEW-monday-high-of-week-profile; 078 ~1:07:29: "an obvious Monday high of the week bearish profile"): weekly profile (template, tag): bearish HTF bias, Monday trades up into a daily SIBI and rejects - Monday's high can be the high of the week; expect Tuesday-Wednesday to deliver lower, often with a lower gap open and a large-range down day (conflicts with T14's 'bearish highs often Tuesday'). `mon_how` "tag" / "gate" (shorts under Monday's high; the tag is cleared by any trade above it); mirror for bullish.
  - Caption-gap fix (NEW-failed-weekly-push-range; 102 ~11:29: "this tends to create a need for consolidation"): last week traded to (or slightly through) a prior weekly wick's CE but neither closed above it nor exceeded the high of two weeks ago: this week is a range from last week's low to that wick's upper quadrant (75%); expect consolidation, no trend/runner trades, until a daily close beyond the upper quadrant, then continuation into the next higher-TF inefficiency; the directional preference is kept; mirror for bearish. `wk_push_range`.
- **T1 Clock.** New York time, daylight saving included; ignore platform GMT dividers (Ep 01;
  023 ~16:40; cc08_002 ~0:31) [both]. Time-based charts only, no Heikin Ashi/range/renko (Ep 03).
  Caution: CC Barchart index/bond charts are Central time (NY = CT + 1h) and some 2016 FX charts are
  MT4 server time, so their clock labels are not NY (cc10_011; cc01_008).
- **T2 Opening prices.** Midnight (true daily open, preferred), 08:30 ("trigger time"), 09:30; 13:30
  for the PM (010 ~38:31, ~56:30; 013 ~4:52) [2023]. After a release, retrace bodies stop at the
  08:30 open (064 ~55:13). 0 GMT open [fx-only] (cc08_008). Bot: `levels.opens`.
- **T3 Kill zones.** London 02:00-05:00 (he uses 01:00-05:00 personally), HOD/LOD usually 02:00-04:00
  (Ep 06; cc08_001; cc08_005 ~3:28); NY 07:00-09:00 (FX) or 07:00-10:00; ICT's futures kill zone
  08:20-11:00 (cc10_007 ~4:35); London close 10:00-12:00 (Ep 06; cc08_002 ~4:32). Asia 20:00-00:00
  is FX only; indices start at midnight (095 ~20:23). For the bot only the London 02:00-05:00
  high/low matters, as a liquidity pool (067).
  - Caption-gap fix (T3-london-close-multiday-reversal; cc09_005 ~32:44: "the London close can time a market reversal"): a reversal at the London close (10:00-12:00 NY) from an HTF PD array can mark a MULTI-DAY turn (several one-sided days), judged with the weekly templates and structure: tag it as a possible multi-day flip. After an expected NY-session reversal the London close may run that reversal's stops one more time at the HTF array - a re-entry chance in the reversal direction. Bot: `lc_tag` (off; `Trade.lc_reversal`: an entry armed in 10:00-12:00 against the 09:30-10:00 leg whose sweep extreme sat INSIDE an HTF Confluence array), `lc_reentry` (off; after a stopped-out lc_reversal ONE more try in the same direction when the new sweep runs beyond the first extreme at an HTF array; bypasses the one-trade-per-session flag for this case only; the gate binds that slot (session + done key) only, the retry must sit in the SAME HTF array as the first trade, and it ends when the retry's trade closes).
  - Caption-gap fix (T3-london-close-counter-trend-scalp; cc09_006 ~00:23:58; cc09_007 ~00:09:29) [taught on FX; ICT no longer trades it - it often fails - and says it suits the S&P better]: London close counter-trend scalp on a bullish day (bearish mirror) when London (00:00-05:00) and NY (07:00-10:00) moved the same way, the day range is >= ADR5 (prefer 1.25x) and the time is 10:30-13:00: on the 5m wait for a high, a pullback and a lower / equal rally (a failure swing), sell the last up-close 5m candle (the bearish OB); stop HOD + buffer (FX 10 pips; index points are ours), target 20-30 % of the day range back from the HOD (~1R max; the '<= 20 pips' is the target cap). Window conflict: T3's London close 10:00-12:00 vs this setup's 10:30-13:00. Bot (off): `lc_scalp` with `lc_mode` "failure_swing", `lc_adr_mult` 1.0, `lc_stop_pts` 2.0, `lc_retrace` 0.25, window `lc_fs_win` (630, 780); own done key LC.
  - Caption-gap fix (new-london-close-scalp-large-range; cc09_005 ~00:12:05) [taught on FX]: the London-close counter-trend scalp only when today's range reached >= 1.25-1.33 x ADR5 on an explosive one-sided move; between 10:00 and 11:00 NY, on signs of weakness (an MSS against the day's move [ours]) or once the projected numbers are filled, scalp a retracement back into the daily range (~20 % of the day range is our assumption); no trade below ADR5 or on a non-explosive move ('one-sided' = the day open within 15 % of the range from the opposite extreme [ours]). Windows: cc09_005 10:00-11:00, cc09_006 10:30-13:00. Bot (off): `lc_scalp` with `lc_mode` "mss", `lc_adr_k` 1.25, `lc_retrace_mss` 0.2, `london_close` (600, 720) (variants (600, 660), (630, 780)), `lc_oneside` 0.15; the normal MSS + first FVG is the trigger, the target 20 % of the day range from the entry, stop beyond the day's extreme, out by the AM exit.
  - Caption-gap fix (M17-london-close-asia-only-reversal-profile; cc09_006 ~00:29:41: "if it's going to be a reversal Market profile then I'm all over London close"): skip London close and Asia trades by default; take a London-close REVERSAL (10:00-12:00) only when price trades into an HTF PD array not yet met at that time (the same reversal-profile condition as the NY session reversal); the retracement scalp above has its own ADR5 condition. An Asia long needs NY to have ended just above an unmitigated HTF discount array without touching it [FX only; the index analogue at midnight is ours - not built]. Bot (off): `lc_rev_need_htf` (an AM sweep whose extreme falls in 10:00-12:00 needs a same-side HTF array that was not traded into before the sweep bar).
  - Caption-gap fix (new-ny-runs-london-extreme-news; 065 ~03:07:01: "always on the heels of a higher medium impact news driver"): bearish day - London (02:00-05:00) made the high and broke down - with no medium / high-impact release treat the NY retrace as a continuation sell with the London high as invalidation; NY runs above the London high and reverses only on a news day. Bullish mirror. Bot (off): `london_ext_guard` (a no-news day: an AM long whose ladder reaches above the London high is dropped, an AM short's stop never lies beyond it; news days unchanged).
  - Caption-gap fix (T3-london-extreme-consolidation; 010 ~39:18: "the largest portion of the day can many times form overnight in London unless we are in consolidations"): if the market has been chopping inside a range since at least the prior session, do not assume the London 02:00-05:00 extreme is the day's high / low - let NY confirm; otherwise expect it in London. Bot (off): `london_ext_consol` (tag `consol_overnight` = the prior RTH range < 0.6 x ADR5 and the overnight inside it [our interpretation]; the London guard and the extreme-in-London test stand down).
  - Caption-gap fix (T3-london-then-ny-reposition; cc12_004 ~7:35-8:25 "if I fail in London I look for a New York open setup to reposition"): with the HTF bullish, look for the long in the London open kill zone (aim for the LOD); if London fails (stopped out or no setup), look for a NY open kill zone setup to reposition or a new long in the same HTF direction (bearish mirror: the HOD). Close most intraday positions 10:00-11:00. Bot (no London entries other than the SB): the NY AM entry plays the reposition role; `london_reposition` (off): a losing `sb_london` trade is no AM session loss / fail (the day-level stops still see it) and the AM re-enters in the HTF direction only.
- **T4 Opening range (OR).** OR = 09:30-10:00 "always"; don't trade it: erratic, the first move is
  often a Judas; bias is read from how it delivers; take an earlier setup only if obvious (029
  ~11:44; 031 ~4:52; 036 ~27:30; 042 ~4:29; 044 ~4:54; 065B ~4:53:40; 092 ~1:45:24 "amateur hour")
  [2023]. Exceptions: entries at 09:37-09:46 with price already deep in discount (034; 041; 053
  ~10:03). CC: OR = 09:30-10:30, tends to hold the HOD or LOD; wide OR -> its opposite end gets run
  (cc10_010 ~3:07-7:05) [CC]. Bot: AM entries from 09:30; `or_no_entry` (orders fill from `or_end`; `or_deep` = the 09:37-09:46
  exception with the entry in the lower quarter of the prior day's range [our interpretation]); `or_bias` (the OR's delivery:
  the first extreme was the Judas / the close vs the midpoint); `or_wide` (trade toward the far end of a wide OR). D1.
  - Caption-gap fix (T4-or-length; 073 ~25:03, cc10_010 ~11:09): OR20 (09:30-09:50) is usable after 20 minutes ("it can form in 20 minutes"; liquidity taken outside it informs the 09:50-10:10 macro's next draw); the 2023 "don't trade the OR" uses OR30; the CC index rules use OR60. Decision `or_minutes` in {20, 30, 60} (30 default); `ctx.or20` is always built.
  - Caption-gap fix (T4-or-needs-bias-iof; cc10_010 ~00:11:25: "When we have the opening range idea defined, and we have bias"): the OR30 / OR60 highs and lows are support / resistance (entry and target levels) together with a defined daily bias and HTF institutional order flow; without both treat them as tags only [our interpretation; ICT does not say "only"].   - Caption-gap fix (LLT3-wide-or-bias-end; cc10_010 ~00:06:15: "we'll look for that upper end of the opening range to be violated or traded to"): OR60 = 09:30-10:30; if it is wide (>= 1.5 x the 20-session median OR60 range [our interpretation]) one end is violated later in the day - the OR60 high on a bullish day, the low on a bearish day, whichever way the OR itself moved (the end is chosen by the daily bias, not by the OR's direction); without a bias no target from this rule. Bot (off): `wide_or_draw` (`wide_or_mult`): after 10:30, in the bias direction, the OR60 end is the draw (pool): the order's final target AND the session lock (`tp3.wide_or_relock` in `_lock_step`, once, while the end is untaken).   - Caption-gap fix (new-hold-pre0930-through-open; 105 ~00:39:20, ~00:44:52: "at 9:30 we're really going to rip up into this area here if it doesn't do before"): a funded pre-09:30 position (partials booked) whose target is untaken may be held through 09:30 with the stop behind the HTF array / bodies; a drift lower into the open (long; mirror for shorts) raises the risk of a 09:30 sweep of the low and the stop - ICT's mitigation is being funded, not reducing size. An unfunded pre-open position is not held through 09:30 under this flag [ours]. Conflicts with T4 'don't trade the OR'. Bot (off): `pre_hold_open` (the last 20 minutes of 5m closes in the lower third of the post-entry range = `risk_0930_sweep`; unfunded and flagged: closed at 09:29).
  - Caption-gap fix (T4-no-early-entry-conditions; 100 ~1:26:48: "this is one of those instances where I wouldn't do it"): disable the early (pre / at 09:30) entry when a second scheduled driver is still pending (e.g. 10:00 news on an 08:30 day) or when untaken pools sit close on both sides, especially beyond the stop ("close" = within the trade's risk distance [our interpretation]). - **T7 Macros** (20-minute timing windows; timing, never direction, 073 ~14:54) [2023]:

    009 ~18:24; 044 ~55:47; 073 ~19:41).

    ~1:10:00).

    ~42:30; 068 ~12:42). "2:00 macro" reversal ~13:39-14:00, only after lunch (015 ~31:57).

    nothing if the HTF target already hit (Ep 01; 014 ~5:19; 030 ~20:06; 032 ~47:41; 056 ~19:08).
    15:00-16:00 holds three macros, usually two occur (022 ~3:16:42).

(A) A REVERSAL point: the run from 09:30 completes inside the window by taking a pool (buy-side in 043 / 073) and rebalancing a premium FVG ('premium' = above 50 % of the 09:30-now range in REAL prices: a real bearish FVG above the EQ after a buy-side run, a real bullish FVG below it after a sell-side run; 'rebalanced' = a body closed inside it and the next bar left it the reversal way [ours]); the window's extreme becomes the swing extreme and the draw flips to the opposite pool when the day's narrative supports it (the NFP profile, 073). (B) A CONTINUATION re-accumulation: price consolidates and leaves an FVG in the bias direction, bought / sold on the retrace toward the next draw (015). (C) The start of a run (the existing 09:50-10:10 macro). T12 uses "true-day" in this sense. DayCtx: `ny_close_1500`, `true_day_hl` (read through dwc2.true_day: nothing before that bar has closed).
